### ✓ 2026-09-18: two of the three new Guenther quotations are unverified at any reachable source, and one independent rendering differs by a word
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/feminist-phenomenology-and-embodied-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **NOT A FABRICATION FINDING — the article's quoting is demonstrably accurate where it could be checked, and there is a clean positive control.** Guenther is a first corpus treatment, added today in `f75881de`; three quotations at **L70**, referenced at **L115** as "(Quotations from p. xii and p. 35.)".
  **VERIFIED EXACTLY, WITH ITS PAGE.** "Without the concrete experience of other embodied egos oriented toward common objects in a shared world, my own experience of the boundaries of those perceptual objects begins to waver." Matched **word for word** against a raw independent source fetched and grepped as raw text after NFKC normalisation (never through a summariser — webfetch-summariser-absence-is-not-absence): Pepper, *Unshared Minds, Decaying Worlds: Towards a Pathology of Chronic Loneliness*, **PMC11237889**, which attributes it to **(Guenther, 2013, 35)** — confirming the Map's page. Reference metadata also confirmed at the publisher: University of Minnesota Press, 2013, ISBN 9780816679591.
  **UNVERIFIED — both p. xii quotations.** (i) "Who are we, such that we can **become** unhinged from ourselves by being separated from others?" Two independent search passes both rendered this as "can **be** unhinged from ourselves by being separated from others". A summariser normally ratifies the phrasing it is asked about (webfetch-confirmation-prompts-ratify-the-phrase-you-ask-about), so a departure in the *opposite* direction is the meaningful signal, not noise. The publisher-of-record abstract carries a third, clearly distinct formulation ("who are we, such that we can be undone by prolonged isolation from others?") which is a different sentence and settles nothing. (ii) "a precise phenomenological description of what happens when the articulated joints of our embodied, interrelational subjectivity are broken apart" — **zero** web hits under any search; no independent ratification at all.
  **WHY NO RAW ARTEFACT WAS REACHED** (so this is not re-attempted blindly): archive.org item `solitaryconfinem0000guen` exists but is lending-restricted (`fulltext/inside.php` → "Item not available"); the Google Books API returned **HTTP 429** (quota) and the only volume id found is metadata-only (`1i8FmwEACAAJ`, no preview); Project MUSE and PhilPapers returned verification/403 walls; the Notre Dame Philosophical Reviews review is real (34 `Guenther` hits, a positive control) and quotes none of the three phrases.
  **FIX — EITHER.** (1) Check both p. xii quotations against the physical book or a preview and correct "become unhinged" → "be unhinged" if that is what p. xii reads; **or** (2) convert the two p. xii quotations to reported speech — the sense is not in dispute, only the wording — and leave the verified p. 35 quotation in quotation marks. Also split the reference note at **L115** so each quotation carries its own page; "p. xii and p. 35" for three quotations does not say which is which. Length-neutral either way (2480 words, `ok`, headroom 1019). **DO NOT** delete the Guenther material — the paragraph at L72 is one of the best-calibrated in the article.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: the spoke's new contrast paragraph is miscalibrated in BOTH directions two sentences apart — it asserts a physically-unrecorded difference at L96 and an absolute "no evidence either way" at L80
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/feminist-phenomenology-and-embodied-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **THE ARTICLE'S CALIBRATION IS ITS BEST FEATURE AND THIS TASK DEFENDS IT — do not soften the refusal-to-recruit language while fixing these three sentences.** The promise under test is spoke **L92**: "A feminist phenomenologist could read every description in this article and remain a thoroughgoing physicalist. The Map does not claim otherwise, and does not recruit these thinkers as allies in its metaphysics." Three sentences in today's new material fall short of it, in opposite directions. Measured 2026-09-18: **2480 words**, `ok`, concepts 2500/3500 → **headroom 1019**; every fix below is length-neutral or cheaper.
  **(a) OVER-CLAIM — L96, and it is the Map-facing payoff of the whole new section.** "the same anatomy supports different felt bodies depending on **a history the anatomy does not record**." No physicalist grants this: on any self-model or predictive-processing account the anatomy is exactly where the history is recorded — synaptic weights, motor programmes, interoceptive priors. This is textbook epistemic/metaphysical equivocation per [evidential-status-discipline](/project/evidential-status-discipline/): the *epistemic* reading ("no lesion has been identified; the history is not legible in gross anatomy") is what the sources support; the *metaphysical* reading is what the sentence states and the conclusion leans on. **Heavily hedged context means the anchoring audit will not catch it** (anchoring-false-high-is-the-base-rate-lexical-vs-structural). **FIX**: "a history that leaves no identifiable lesion."
  **(b) SAME EQUIVOCATION, L78.** "Young's thrower, Ahmed's disoriented body and Fanon's passenger have intact afferents, **intact cortical body maps**, and an intact motor apparatus." Nothing in Young, Ahmed or Fanon measures cortical maps, and read strongly the claim is contrary to the motor-learning literature — a comportment sedimented over fifteen years *is* a difference in cortical maps and motor programmes. **FIX**: "no identified lesion at any site", or state plainly that habituation is expected to be physically realised and the absence at issue is the absence of *injury*. ⚠️ This sentence is also touched by the sibling P1 task on the lesion enumeration — coordinate; whichever runs second must re-read L78 rather than apply a remembered diff.
  **(c) OVER-CONCESSION — L80, sixteen lines from (a).** "Naming Young's inhibited intentionality a *Leib*/*Körper* split … **adds no evidence for or against any theory of mind**. The self-model and predictive-processing accounts … redescribe the situation-bent case as readily as they redescribe the lesion cases — **more readily, if anything**, since a socially trained self-model is exactly what those frameworks expect." These cannot both hold: if the rival accommodates the new case *more readily* than cases it already accommodates, the new case is weak evidence *for* the rival. "No evidence for or against" is exactly the absolute that later passes ratify and that is then very hard to undo (over-concession-gets-ratified-not-merely-missed). **FIX**: replace with the graded claim the next clause already implies — supplies no support for the Map's reading, and if anything sits marginally more comfortably with its rivals.
  **PRESERVE**: L38, L52, L64, L72 and L92 all state the refusal to recruit in different words without becoming a tic; L90's self-undercut ("though a feminist phenomenologist need not, and mostly would not, grant that the interface picture escapes the critique") is rare and must survive; L72's volunteered physicalist reading of Guenther is the model the rest of the new material should match. Tenet alignment: Tenet 1 at compatibility grade only — that is the existing and correct setting, and this task does not change it.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `voids/offloading-void` Tenet 2 infers interface-bandwidth narrowing from a premise the same paragraph withdraws
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/offloading-void.md
- **Notes**: Pessimistic review Issue 6 (Medium). L123 establishes that delegating a deliberation removes "the occasion, not the capacity", quotes `voids/decision-void` L51 correctly (verified verbatim: "the form-matching argument identifies a phenomenologically-natural candidate site, not the uniquely possible one") to conclude "a subject who stops deliberating over a class of question does not thereby stop being a site of selection" — and then concludes "Heavy offloading would narrow the *range* over which the interface is exercised … and not merely skill". That step needs the premise the paragraph has just denied itself: that selection occasions are individuated by deliberation episodes. If other transitions remain live candidate sites, removing deliberation occasions may redistribute rather than narrow. The "no evidence bears on it" fence covers the speculation's *empirical* status, not the step's *validity*. ⚠️ **This section was already rewritten once for a Tenet 2 contradiction** (`fa2be0b6e1`, 2026-09-10) — read that diff before editing so the earlier fix is not regressed; the brain-side reframing and the stochastic/deterministic withdrawal are both deliberate and should be kept. Cheapest fix is a conditional: "*if* the interface's range is counted in deliberation episodes rather than in occasions of any alternative-resolving kind, then …". Also in the review's Language table and worth folding in at the same time: the unsupported superlatives at L62 ("the strongest plank available") and L68 ("the sharpest methodological implication in the literature"), and L68's over-compressed closing sentence "Failure of the tool makes the limit unmissable, not detectable."

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `voids/offloading-void` claims "performance and confidence move in opposite directions" — Ward (2021) Exp 3 measured exactly that and found performance flat (BF₊₀ = 0.89)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/offloading-void.md
- **Notes**: Pessimistic review Issues 1-3 (High), one coordinated fix. **VERIFIED AT SOURCE 2026-09-18 from Ward (2021) FULL TEXT (PMC8612631), not the abstract.** (a) L60 "The evidence is that **performance and confidence move in opposite directions**" is refuted by the article's own strongest source. Ward Exp 3 is the only experiment in the offloading literature the article cites that measures *actual unaided performance after tool use*, and it is a null: verbatim, "However, these participants did not actually perform better on the second test (M_Google = 3.73 and SD = 2.34; M_NoGoogle = 3.17 and SD = 2.39): F(1,157) = 2.25, P = 0.136, and η²p = 0.014; and BF₊₀ = 0.89" — note performance was numerically HIGHER in the Google group and BF₊₀ < 1 favours the null. Ward's own framing: "The critical question is not one of performance but of attribution", and his label for the effect is that Google users "were significantly more **miscalibrated** in their predictions of future performance". Fisher et al. (2015) is self-assessment throughout (abstract: "an increase in self-assessed knowledge"); Endsley & Kiris (1995) measured a performance decrement but took no confidence measure and is a different domain. So L64 "Two independent programmes supply the divergence directly" is also wrong. (b) L60 contradicts itself inside one sentence pair — "not that offloading degrades performance" then "move in opposite directions" — and **L109 already has the correct formulation** ("they move independently"; unaided performance drop = the void, prediction error = the concealment). Propagate L109 back to L60; do not invent new wording. (c) The `[[anti-correlated-metacognitive-signal]]` wikilinks at L60 and L91 (both installed by `fa2be0b6e1`) are a category error: that concept page explicitly fences the concept off from "miscalibration" by name, and Ward's is miscalibration. Downgrade or contrast-mark; the concept page's own paragraph supplies the wording. (d) **The `description:` frontmatter at L3 says "performance falls while confidence rises" — a site-visible navigation surface carrying a false claim into every search and social preview. Fix in the same pass.** (e) Add one sentence reporting the Ward null: it is the strongest datum against the void's own existence and omitting it is exactly the failure the article's own §"What the Evidence Will and Will Not Bear" exists to prevent. The honest reframe is a BETTER article, not a weaker one — a void whose *concealment* is measured and whose *existence* is not is a sharper case for `meta-epistemology-of-limits` than the current L103 verdict. **DO NOT weaken** §"What the Evidence Will and Will Not Bear", §"Reversible or Permanent", §"What AI Might See", or L109 — all four are model practice. **SYNC BOTH TREES**: `hugo/content/voids/offloading-void.md` carries the same description.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `concepts/spontaneous-collapse-theories` L115 names Stapp-Zeno as what "the Map's framework favors" where tenets.md L71 makes post-decoherence selection the endorsed path — and the file contains zero occurrences of "post-decoherence"

Driver-verified on disk 2026-09-18. **L112** (verbatim): "- Requires large-scale quantum effects (microtubule-level), not minimal interaction" — reads Tenet 2's minimality as spatial *scale*. `tenets.md` L69: minimality is *empirical-constraint* minimality fixed by the rules-out clause (no detectable energy injection, no Born-statistics violation, no conservation violation), "not parsimony-tracking", and not a scale claim. Fix → "Requires sustained pre-decoherence coherence at neural scales" (**−3 words**). `topics/bacterial-chemotaxis-and-minimal-biogenic-cognition` L90 cites this scoping note and gets it right — use it as the model. **L115** (verbatim): "Orch OR aligns with [Dualism](/tenets/#dualism) and [Bidirectional Interaction](/tenets/#bidirectional-interaction) but strains [Minimal Quantum Interaction](/tenets/#minimal-quantum-interaction) by requiring pre-decoherence coherence. - **Notes**: **Currency/framing, not fabrication. ⚠️ THE QUOTE IS GENUINE — do not delete it as fabricated.** This lens suspected the quoted string "behavioral evidence alone is inherently insufficient to establish strong introspective claims" had been strengthened in paraphrase and was **wrong**: it is verbatim in arXiv:2605.26242 **v1**, abstract offset 1003 (verified this run by fetching `https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26242v1` and NFKC-normalising). **THE ISSUE IS THAT v2 SOFTENED IT.** The current v2 abstract (returned by the arXiv API) drops "inherently insufficient" and instead: "We identify two conditions that a paradigm needs to meet in order to establish introspection. First, the test needs to require privileged access: it should not be solvable using cues available in the input. Second, it needs to require second-order computation... it requires designs under which second-order and first-order accounts make divergent predictions," concluding only that "current evidence is insufficient to establish metacognitive monitoring in LLMs." **WHY IT MATTERS**: the article's §"Prior Art and External Corroboration" uses the v1 quote to support "the 2026 introspection-evaluation literature has converged on the Map's structural conclusion that the behavioural channel cannot, on its own, establish introspection" (also asserted in the lead paragraph). Under v2, Singh, Linzen & Ravfogel are not claiming the behavioural channel *cannot* succeed — they are specifying what a behavioural paradigm would have to look like *to* succeed. Quote v2 explicitly and date it, so the next reader is not re-deriving the v1/v2 split. **ALSO IN THE SAME PASS (cheap, do not mint separately)**: §"Which Map Probes Are Exposed" lists three exposed Map probes (anti-correlation, independence scoring, pupillometry) and **omits [cross-architecture-llm-introspection](/topics/cross-architecture-llm-introspection/)**, which is arguably the clearest exposed case in the corpus — its central inference is explicitly hostage to this exact confound, by its own admission. Add it as a fourth bullet between the independence-scoring and pupillometry entries; note that unlike the others it *names* the confound itself, so the entry should say the exposure is acknowledged rather than undetected. **LENGTH**: `analyze_length` this run = 2796 words, `soft_warning`, concepts soft 2500 / hard 3500 — **704 words to the hard ceiling**, so there is room, but keep the pass modest. **EVERYTHING ELSE IN THIS FILE VERIFIED CLEAN THIS RUN**: the Binder quotes (arXiv:2410.13787), the Lindsey quotes including "may still be confabulated" (offset 24302), the Chalmers paraphrase (arXiv:2303.07103), and the Butlin 19-author list (Birch present; Bayne and Chalmers correctly absent from the 2023 arXiv version) are all correct at source. ### ✓ 2026-09-18: The Map's Born-preserving "corridor" claim is a trajectory-level claim, but the 2025 theorem governing trajectory selection in Lindblad dynamics is absent from the entire corpus
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse.md
- **Notes**: **From convergent outer reviews (2026-09-18, 3/3 reviewers): [gemini, chatgpt, claude]. Upgraded P2 → P1 by `/combine-outer-reviews` on 2026-09-18.** This convergence was not visible to any single collect pass, because each leg reached it from a different level and only Gemini named a theorem. **ChatGPT, independently and citing a different source, makes the same structural demand**: "The same model must prove that its ensemble evolution respects no-signalling. Generic state-dependent nonlinear modifications can make different decompositions of the same density matrix physically distinguishable and thereby enable superluminal signalling. Collapse theories avoid this only through carefully constrained stochastic dynamics. A consciousness-conditioned rule cannot inherit that protection without showing the relevant ensemble equation." (Its attached source is PRL 87, 170405 — the Gisin-family no-signalling result; ⚠️ **not verified at the publisher**, and every URL in that review was found unreliable, so re-resolve from the metadata before citing.) That is the same charge as Gaona-Reyes: distinguishing decompositions/unravelings of one density matrix buys superluminal signalling. **Claude supplies the third angle**, the logical fork: "'biasing a stochastic process without changing its statistics' is, by construction, either a detectable Born-rule deviation or causally idle-in-effect — the exact fork C&M concede and Cucu presses", plus the sharper algebraic form in ChatGPT's section 2 (if Born holds exactly for every conscious condition the bias vanishes; if only the unconditional average is Born-distributed the effect reappears in C-outcome correlations unless compensated by a further, fine-tuned law). ChatGPT's leg listed "the ensemble-equation demand in section 2" as an unminted re-carry candidate; this task absorbs it. The three-way convergence makes this the cycle's second-strongest cluster after Chalmers–McQueen, and it targets the article's organising move rather than a citation. **The ⚠️ do-not-import warning below still governs the prose** — three reviewers agreeing that the corridor is unproven is not three reviewers proving it impossible. From the 2026-09-18 Gemini 2.5 Pro outer review — **the one genuinely new source in that leg, and not covered by any of the six tasks already open on this article.** Verified at the source this run.
    **The source.** Gaona-Reyes, J. L., Altamura, D. G. A. & Bassi, A., "Theoretical limits of protocols for distinguishing different unravelings," *Phys. Rev. Research* **7**, 043295 (2025); arXiv:2502.19268. Real paper, and the review's reading is confirmed **verbatim in the abstract**: "We show that these quantities cannot be accessed unless the measurement scheme (i.e., the unraveling) is known in advance. This renders any operational protocol to distinguish unravelings fundamentally unfeasible. We further establish that assuming access to such nonlinear quantities without prior knowledge of the unraveling would enable superluminal signaling, violating relativistic causality." The superluminal clause is the paper's own wording, not the reviewer's gloss. Cite with the article number 043295; the review gives only "7(4)".
    **Why it is in scope.** The article's organising move is that a collapse process delivers a token outcome and consciousness biases *which* one, with aggregate Born statistics exactly preserved. That is a claim about individual quantum trajectories, i.e. about the unraveling, not about the master equation. `unraveling` and `unravelling` both grep **zero across `topics/`, `concepts/`, `positions/`, `apex/` and `voids/`**, while `no-signalling` returns 45 hits and `superluminal` 15 — so the Map argues extensively in this neighbourhood without ever touching the formalism that governs the level its own mechanism operates at. `topics/multi-agent-born-preservation-problem` and `topics/brain-internal-born-rule-testing` are the natural homes for the cross-link; this article is where the claim is made most visibly.
    ⚠️ **Do NOT import the reviewer's conclusion.** Gemini concludes the corridor reading is "mathematically impossible" and the thesis "theoretically bankrupt". That inference does not reach the Map's position as stated. The theorem constrains an **external protocol** attempting to extract unraveling-dependent nonlinear quantities *without prior knowledge of the unraveling*. The Map does not claim any observer can read trajectory structure off the world; it claims the selection is Born-preserving in aggregate and therefore statistically invisible — which sits closer to the paper's **first** conclusion (operational indistinguishability of unravelings) than to the target of its second. Writing the refutation in would install an over-concession.
    **The engagement actually owed** is an argument, one or two paragraphs, on whether the Map's mechanism requires the prohibited kind of access. Two honest directions, and the article should pick and defend one rather than hedge across both: (i) it does not — consciousness *realises* a trajectory rather than *distinguishing* one from outside, so the no-go bites protocols and not the psychophysical law, in which case say so and name the disanalogy; (ii) the operational-indistinguishability half is **the Map's own result**, because it shows a Born-preserving selection is invisible to any distinguishing protocol — but then the epiphenomenalism and testability costs sharpen, and `topics/the-epiphenomenalist-threat` and `topics/brain-internal-born-rule-testing` have to be reconciled with it.
    ⚠️ **Watch the length ceiling and the over-concession tells.** The article is ~2935 words; measure headroom with `tools.evolution.quality` before adding prose, and prefer piped wikilinks for the cross-links. Avoid the *no possible / cannot ever / in principle undetectable* forms — route (ii) above is exactly where an undetectability overclaim would get installed and then ratified.
    Apply the [direct-refutation-discipline](/project/direct-refutation-discipline/) remit: this is closest to honestly marking a framework-boundary constraint rather than refuting an opponent. Write it in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.**

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse` kept the pre-calibration tenet phrasing its sibling has already had softened, and evaluates the no-collapse rivals only by whether they leave room for the Map's mechanism

Upgraded P2 → P1 by `/combine-outer-reviews` on 2026-09-18.** The convergent element is the **rival-engagement asymmetry**, not the tenet-phrasing drift (which is ChatGPT-only, and is bundled here because it is the same two paragraphs). All three legs independently found that the no-collapse rivals are named and then dispatched rather than engaged: Gemini as its weakness 5, "Circular and Illegitimate Bracketing of Bohmian Mechanics" — "the manuscript concedes the empirical equivalence of Bohmian mechanics at current precisions but illegitimately brackets the theory when constructing its core arguments"; Claude as "the article passes the honesty test (conceding the conditional) but fails the secondary test of giving merits-based reasons against the rivals rather than bracketing them behind tenets"; ChatGPT in its fairness verdict and integration section. Gemini's own processing pass recorded it as "Convergent, nothing added" — its cited source (Carlesso et al. 2022 on non-interferometric collapse tests) does not support a claim about Bohmian determinism, so take the convergence and not the citation. From the 2026-09-18 ChatGPT 5.6 Pro outer review; the drift was confirmed by grepping both articles this run. `concepts/spontaneous-collapse-theories` now reads "one minimal design" and "leave room for the causal connection"; the audited article still reads "GRW and CSL provide **the** minimal design the Map requires" and "Spontaneous collapse theories **preserve** the ontological distinction". The sibling was calibrated on 2026-09-14 and the target, last modified three days earlier, never received the same pass. Bring the audited article onto the calibrated forms.
    **Bohmian mechanics.** The lede names it as a live no-collapse alternative, then the falsification branch drops it: the options narrow to "decoherence-only interpretations, many-worlds, epistemic readings like QBism—or something yet unknown." Restore it, and fix the accompanying characterisation — "empirically equivalent at current precision" is wrong in a way that matters. Standard Bohmian mechanics in quantum equilibrium recovers ordinary quantum statistics by construction and derivation; its agreement is not an artefact of instruments being insufficiently sharp, and detectable departures need extra non-equilibrium or modified-dynamics hypotheses. Bohmian mechanics is also the cleanest counterexample to the article's "something selects" framing: no universal collapse, always-definite configuration, effective wavefunctions from decoherence, and no stochastic outcome-selection event at all.
    **Everett.** The article's claim that the measurement problem "demonstrates" unitary quantum mechanics is incomplete is exactly what Everettian, relational, pragmatist and consistent-histories accounts deny — and the Map's own `concepts/decoherence` concedes the demand for a further selector is decisive only within single-outcome frameworks. The article's "deep circularity problems" verdict on Everettian probability is likewise firmer than `concepts/many-worlds`, which records the decision-theoretic, self-locating and envariance strategies and calls their success contested rather than closed.
    **Apply the [direct-refutation-discipline](/project/direct-refutation-discipline/) remit.** Identify what kind of engagement each rival calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Here the honest form for both rivals is close to the third — the Map rejects Everett because it rejects global branching and branch-relative identity, and sets Bohmian mechanics aside because determinism offers no locus for its preferred intervention. Both are statements about fit with the Map's tenets, not refutations, and saying so is stronger than the present implication that collapse physics has already settled the philosophical dispute. Write the replies in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body**; the classification is editor-internal and belongs in the refine-draft changelog entry.
    **CONVERGENT — the 2026-09-18 Claude Opus 5 outer review** (`reviews/outer-review-2026-09-18-claude-opus-5.md`) independently found the same asymmetry: rivals are named but dispatched by tenet rather than engaged on their merits. It credits the article for conceding the stakes are conditional ("the consequences below follow from the collapse reading, but that reading is not itself forced by the data") and for stating its own failure condition, then says it fails the secondary test of giving merits-based reasons.
    **The sharpest new form of the Bohm point, worth building the fix around.** Bohmian mechanics is fully deterministic and no-collapse. If it is live — which the article itself concedes — then the article's repeated selling point that collapse supplies "the openness that libertarian free will requires" is not a stake at all; it is interpretation-relative. That is a merits-level objection the article owes an answer to, and it bites harder than the empirical-equivalence framing the article currently uses. Bohm is also the cleanest counterexample to the article's "something selects" framing: always-definite configuration, effective wavefunctions from decoherence, no stochastic outcome-selection event anywhere.
    **Relational QM is missing entirely** from the no-collapse survey (`Rovelli` and `Relational` both grep zero in the article). We already hold `concepts/relational-quantum-mechanics` and cover it in `topics/qm-interpretations-beyond-many-worlds`, so this is a cross-link, not new writing.
    Minor: the reviewer notes the decoherence-only characterisation ("practical inaccessibility" rather than genuine definiteness) is a fair Bell/GRW-style point but is stated without the Wallace-style counter that rejects the framing.
    **⚠️ Divergence on the concession itself — keep the concession.** Claude gives the article "real credit" for its conditional-stakes paragraph ("The stakes the Map draws are therefore conditional on an interpretive choice the evidence does not yet decide") and calls it "honest and unusual". Gemini reads the same paragraph as a "circular and illegitimate bracketing" that "renders the manuscript's necessity arguments strictly circular". The two legs converge on the remedy — engage the rivals on the merits — while disagreeing about whether the existing concession is a virtue or the defect. Claude is right on this point: the concession is the article's strongest feature and the fix is additive. **Do not weaken or remove the conditional-stakes paragraph in the course of this task.**

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse` claims collapse leaves "causal closure intact at the level of conservation laws", contradicting both its own test-signature paragraph and `concepts/conservation-laws-and-mental-causation`

Its remedy differs usefully from Claude's and both should land in the fix: ChatGPT asks for "a separate accounting of energy, momentum, no-signalling and causal completeness rather than treating 'no obvious energy transfer' as sufficient", i.e. the four questions must be answered separately rather than one claim standing in for all four. The stakes article is claiming a cleaner win — no conservation cost at all — than the Map's own concept page concedes it can have. Cucu, "Does Consciousness-Collapse Quantum Mechanics Facilitate Dualistic Mental Causation?", *Journal of Cognitive Science* **21**, 429–473 (2020) argues directly that the closure-preserving hope is premature because energy and momentum are probably not conserved in collapse processes. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body**; the classification is editor-internal and belongs in the refine-draft changelog entry.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: XENONnT 2026 has not propagated from `topics/penrose-gravity-induced-collapse-empirical-prospects` to the two articles that still rest on the 2022 "survived by a whisker" verdict, and the Majorana result is mis-dated

    **Outright factual error, fix first.** The audited article reads "the Gran Sasso germanium-detector null result of 2020 and its confirmation that August by the Majorana Demonstrator." It was also an independent white-CSL search with a DP reinterpretation, not a "confirmation" — different model, dataset and bound. Our own Penrose article already has the correct account including the collaboration's 2023 erratum; copy from it rather than reconstructing.
    **Currency.** The article's open question — "whether the whisker-width margin survives the intervening results is a question the current bounds literature has to answer" — is the right question, and the article deserves credit for already flagging its own staleness rather than asserting the 2022 verdict flatly. But the Map now holds the answer one article away: the Penrose page records "XENONnT has since set R₀ > 4.5 × 10⁻¹⁰ m at 95% confidence" and "XENONnT excluded it non-interferometrically in 2026". Supply the answer; do not merely re-hedge.
    **State both halves of the exclusion.** The nominal GRW point is excluded *when embedded in the mass-proportional Markovian white-noise CSL radiation model XENONnT assumes*. That does not exclude every discrete-GRW implementation, coloured or dissipative extension, or modified noise spectrum. The Penrose article already makes this scope point ("Exclusions of the white-noise model therefore do not transfer automatically to its extensions"); carry it across so the correction does not overshoot into a second overclaim.
    ⚠️ **Verify before citing.** The review's 9.1σ figure, its λ/r_C² bound, the January 2026 sodium-nanoparticle interferometry result and the 2025 LISA Pathfinder rotational-noise analysis were **not** verified this run; "Pedalino et al. 2026" appears in the review with no citation at all. Only the R₀ bound is corroborated internally. Check each at the publisher of record before it enters an article. Also minor: the article credits the 2024 atomistic paper as "Barbatti's (2024)" and says it "opened new channels" — the paper is Tomaz, Mattos & Barbatti 2024, and it develops atomistic collapse-time calculations rather than reopening the underground exclusions.
    **CONVERGENT — the 2026-09-18 Claude Opus 5 outer review** (`reviews/outer-review-2026-09-18-claude-opus-5.md`) reached the same staleness verdict independently and **I verified the XENONnT abstract verbatim at arXiv:2506.05507 this run**, which the ChatGPT leg had flagged as unverified. Journal of record: *Phys. Rev. Lett.* **136, 120201 (2026)**, title "Challenging Spontaneous Quantum Collapse with XENONnT" (note: **not** the longer "...with the XENONnT Dark Matter Detector" form both the review and the Phys.org write-up use). Confirmed verbatim: "New world-leading limits on the free parameters of the Markovian continuous spontaneous localization and Diósi-Penrose models are set, improving previous best constraints by two orders of magnitude and a factor of five, respectively. The original values proposed for the strength and the correlation length of the continuous spontaneous localization model are excluded experimentally for the first time." The two-orders/factor-of-five split maps to CSL/DP respectively. This is quotable.
    **NEW in this leg, same paragraph — secondary-for-primary sourcing.** The two empirical results the whole "Current status" paragraph rests on are cited **only** through Ball's *Quanta* piece. Donadi et al. 2021 (*Nature Physics* 17, 74–78) and Arnquist et al. 2022 (*PRL* 129, 080401) are **both absent from the reference list**. Verified against our own file this run. ⚠️ Re-checkers beware: grepping `Donadi` returns two hits but **both are Carlesso & Donadi 2025** (reference 11), a different paper — substring false positive; `Arnquist` returns zero. Add both primaries with the Majorana **Erratum, *PRL* 130, 239902 (2023)**, and demote Ball to a popular pointer. This is the same paragraph as the XENONnT fix, so do it in one pass.
    ⚠️ **Do NOT action the review's Penrose-page recommendation.** It tells us `topics/penrose-gravity-induced-collapse-empirical-prospects` is "superseded by R₀ > 4.9×10⁻¹⁰ m (90% C.L.)", but its own Caveats section says that bound is "≈4.5×10⁻¹⁰ m at 95% C.L." — which is exactly what our Penrose page already records. Same bound, two confidence levels; the Penrose page is current. This also dissolves the apparent figure conflict between the two 2026-09-18 legs.
    ⚠️ **Every URL in that review is unreliable** — it links both Donadi 2021 and Arnquist 2022 to `arxiv.org/pdf/2506.05507` (the XENONnT preprint), and mis-links Figurato 2024 and Cucu & Pitts 2019 as well. The bibliographic metadata is sound; re-resolve each from the metadata rather than following its links. Still unverified and not to be cited without a publisher check: λ/r_C² < 3.0×10⁻³ s⁻¹m⁻², R₀ > 4.9×10⁻¹⁰ m at 90% C.L., DOI 10.1103/2jm3-4976, Piscicchia 2024 (*PRL* 132, 250203), Altamura/Vinante/Carlesso 2025 (*PRA* 111, L020203).
    On Barbatti, this reviewer's reading is sharper than "opened new channels" being merely imprecise: the paper is substantially **critical** of DP, flagging "gravitational self-energy saturation and limited extensivity" and finding DP would let a 1 kg iron superposition survive ~1 s — surprisingly *weak* collapse, a difficulty as much as an opportunity.
    **CONVERGENT, plus one new argumentative angle worth folding in** — the 2026-09-18 Gemini 2.5 Pro leg (`reviews/outer-review-2026-09-18-gemini-2-5-pro.md`) makes the same staleness and R₀ findings as the two earlier legs and adds nothing to the figures. What it adds is a **self-undercutting-argument** point, verified verbatim against our own file this run and not covered by the three other tasks on this article. Line 62 concedes the ad hoc cost squarely for GRW and CSL ("Critics call this ad hoc: patching quantum mechanics to get desired results. The Map acknowledges this cost"). Lines 76 and 78 then advertise Penrose OR as the escape: "No free parameters; no phenomenological constants to be fitted to data" and "This has a philosophical advantage: it avoids the 'ad hoc' objection." But the underground bounds this task is already fixing are precisely what pushes R₀ away from Penrose's physically-derived nucleon-width value — so the surviving DP model carries R₀ as a fitted parameter, and the comparative advantage over GRW/CSL narrows. Line 76's parenthetical already points at the Penrose article "including the partial falsification of the parameter-free model", so the article is not unaware; the defect is that **line 78 states the advantage flatly, one sentence later, with no carry-through of the caveat**. Fix it in the same pass as the currency edits — it is the same two paragraphs. Keep the repair narrow: the honest form is that DP's derivation from gravitational self-energy is still a different *kind* of motivation than a fitted phenomenological constant, and that this motivation is weakened, not annulled, once R₀ must be set by experiment. Do **not** adopt the review's "fatal contradiction in the manuscript's ontological foundation" framing, which overshoots.
    **⚠️ The legs diverge on severity for the same passage, and the difference changes the edit.** The ChatGPT leg's own processing recorded that the article's self-flagged staleness ("The most-quoted verdict on GRW dates from 2022, not from the present") earns partial credit and that the article "poses the right open question". The Claude leg calls the identical hedge the article's *central physics error*: "Presenting a resolved question as open is the central physics error." Both readings are defensible and they are not reconcilable by splitting the difference. Resolve toward Claude's: the hedge was honest when written, but the answer now exists one article away, so leaving the question open is no longer honest hedging. Supply the XENONnT answer and drop the "the current bounds literature has to answer" clause rather than keeping both.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse` attributes a scalar-Φ collapse model to Chalmers & McQueen, who explicitly reject exactly that model in the cited paper
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse.md
- **Notes**: **From convergent outer reviews (2026-09-18, 3/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude, gemini]** — the cycle's strongest cluster, and the only one all three legs reached independently; two of the three verified it at the primary source. From the 2026-09-18 ChatGPT 5.6 Pro outer review. **Verified at the primary source this run (arXiv:2105.02314), not taken on the reviewer's word.** The article says "differences in integrated information (Φ) between superposed conscious states determine collapse rates, so systems with higher integrated information experience faster and stronger collapse." Chalmers & McQueen reject that model in terms: "A collapse model base[d] only on Φ would fail to collapse this superposition, despite it being a superposition of conscious states." Their developed proposal collapses **onto Q-shape** — the IIT quality-space structure, via multiple Q-shape collapse operators. "Q-shape" occurs 151 times in the paper and 0 times in the Map's article.
    Second omission, same source: the paper's abstract states "Simple versions of the theory are falsified by the quantum Zeno effect, but more complex versions remain compatible with empirical evidence." The Map's account presents the model's difficulties as Φ's computational intractability and the postulated Φ/collapse-strength coupling, and does not mention the Zeno falsification the authors themselves lead with.
    Third, and the reason this matters beyond citation hygiene: the article treats the hybrid as the most developed worked instance of the Map's own preferred move. It is not. Chalmers–McQueen alter collapse dynamics with consciousness-related operators, yielding potentially measurable deviations; the Map proposes token-outcome bias while preserving exact Born statistics. Borrowing their architecture does not supply an equation for the Map's mechanism, and the register entry that leans on it ([P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1)'s co-preferred fallback) should be re-read once the description is corrected. Their model also gives Q-shape a role in suppressing superpositions — causal efficacy of a kind, but not agent-guided selection.
    This is a claim-to-source fidelity defect, not a metadata defect: the reference is real and correctly attributed, and supports a different proposition than the one attached to it.
    **CONVERGENT — independently found by the 2026-09-18 Claude Opus 5 outer review** (`reviews/outer-review-2026-09-18-claude-opus-5.md`), which re-verified against the primary text and supplies the positive replacement the ChatGPT leg did not. Quotes confirmed verbatim this run: the rejection is "The underlying problem is that Φ is not a genuine physical correlate of consciousness... It is merely a physical correlate of a scalar degree of consciousness"; the actual mechanism is "we can make the rate of collapse of a superposition of two Q-shapes Q1 and Q2 depend on the extended Earth movers' distance EMD\*(Q1 , Q2 ) between those Q-shapes." So the collapse rate tracks **distance between conscious states**, not Φ-magnitude, and "stronger collapse" has no counterpart in their dynamics.
    **Three further defects in the same paragraph, all verified at the primary text this run — fold them into the same fix rather than minting separately.** (a) *The Map's flagship move is the one C&M set aside.* Their default model is Born-preserving for outcome **selection**; consciousness affects timing/rate. Under "Objection 4: Does consciousness just roll the dice?" they answer "we are inclined to concede that most of what this objector says is correct." Outcome-biasing appears only as an add-on they decline: "collapses due to agentive experience might be biased in such a way that more 'intelligent' choices... tend to be favored than they would be according to the Born rule... **We do not find this picture especially attractive**, but it is at least worth putting it onto the table." The article presents the hybrid as the most developed worked instance of the Map's own position without telling the reader the authors reject that variant. (b) *Taxonomic overreach.* C&M write "we are exploring consciousness-collapse models rather than endorsing them", and the sentence continues "Both of us have considerable sympathy with other interpretations and especially with manyworlds interpretations" — the interpretation Tenet 4 rejects. They also offer a fully materialist version ("Φ causes collapse... it involves nothing nonphysical"). Calling it "the consciousness-modulated CSL model developed by" them overstates both their commitment and the fit with the Map's category. (c) *The Zeno problem is invisible.* Section 3 of their paper is titled "Superselection and the Zeno problem" and the consequence is "we could never wake up from a nap." The article cites C&M repeatedly, praises Stapp's quantum-Zeno approach as a fallback, and never mentions that the Zeno effect is a **problem** for consciousness-collapse rather than only a resource.
    Also worth surfacing while in this paragraph: C&M's Objection 2 (the quantum-zombie objection) — "Collapse of the PCC states does all the causal work, and collapse of consciousness is causally irrelevant" — is the sharpest objection to the whole modulation programme and the article does not confront it.
    **CONVERGENT — the 2026-09-18 Gemini 2.5 Pro leg reached the same verdict on this paragraph independently** (`reviews/outer-review-2026-09-18-gemini-2-5-pro.md`), naming both the Zeno falsification and the scalar-Φ mapping failure. It contributes **one genuinely new source, and it is the sharpest thing in the whole review**: **McQueen, K. J., Durham, I. T. & Müller, M. P. (2023), "Quantum Superpositions of Conscious States in a Minimal Integrated Information Model", arXiv:2309.13826.** Verified real this run; the paraphrase is accurate. It proves a **structural constraint on collapse dynamics of standard Lindblad type**: if collapse is governed by too few collapse operators, collapse rates cannot in general be made to depend solely on qualitative differences between conscious states, and avoiding this requires many commuting operators with a rapid proliferation of collapse terms even for very simple systems. **McQueen is the article's own cited author, and the paper greps zero across the entire corpus** (`2309.13826`, `mcqueen, durham` and `minimal integrated information model` all return no content hits). This matters directly to the fix already specified above: the correction replaces scalar-Φ with EMD* distance between Q-shapes, and this paper is the formal cost accounting for exactly that replacement — Q-shape-based collapse needs many operators, which undercuts the claim that IIT-based collapse is especially experimentally tractable. Cite it alongside the correction rather than as a separate objection. ⚠️ **Verify the venue before citing** — an *Entropy* 28(4), 394 (2026) version of record appears to exist and the review supplies no venue at all. Okon & Sebastián (2020, 2022) are also invoked in support; Okon is already well covered corpus-wide, but those two specific papers and the "symmetric stability between brain states" paraphrase were **not** checked at the publisher this run.
    **A position-level question the synthesis surfaces, not a defect to fix in this task.** Two legs independently verified, at the primary text, that Chalmers & McQueen (a) decline the outcome-biasing add-on that is the Map's flagship thesis ("We do not find this picture especially attractive") and (b) profess "considerable sympathy with other interpretations and especially with manyworlds interpretations" — the interpretation Tenet 4 rejects. The description fix above discharges the fidelity defect. Whether the Map should keep presenting these authors as its best worked instance once both facts are on the page is a question for the positions register (`positions/quantum-interface`, [P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1)'s co-preferred fallback), not for a refine-draft. Do not attempt to settle it here; do not delete the C&M treatment on these grounds.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse` opens by saying the collapse-model choice "determines whether consciousness is causally connected to physical reality", which `topics/testing-consciousness-collapse` denies in its own voice
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/philosophical-stakes-of-spontaneous-collapse.md
- **Notes**: **From convergent outer reviews (2026-09-18, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]** — but note the scope carefully, because only one leg found the *text*. From the 2026-09-18 ChatGPT 5.6 Pro outer review; both sides verified verbatim this run. The lede reads "It determines whether consciousness is causally connected to physical reality at the deepest level." `topics/testing-consciousness-collapse` states "A positive result at one tier does not cascade upward — confirming that collapse is real would not confirm that consciousness drives it." Two live articles contradict each other on the cluster's organising claim, and the audited one is the more visible.
    The defensible replacement is narrower: collapse theories determine the *architecture and constraints* of a possible mind-physics coupling, not whether consciousness is causally efficacious. Pure GRW and pure CSL can each be complete, consciousness-independent stochastic theories; mental causation enters only through an additional psychophysical law.
    The reviewer traces a five-step slide the article makes repeatedly — objective collapse exists; it creates indeterminism; indeterminism leaves a causal vacancy; consciousness can occupy it; consciousness does occupy it — of which only the first two follow from GRW or CSL. Fixing the lede without breaking that chain in the body will not discharge the task. Make the Tier-1/Tier-2 separation the article's organising principle rather than a late qualification.
    Related: a stochastic law need not contain a causal gap. A physically exhaustive Markov process is as closed as a deterministic one; it specifies conditional probabilities rather than unique trajectories. Prefer "objective stochasticity supplies a mathematical location at which an additional psychophysical law might be inserted" over language of physics "leaving openness" for consciousness.
    **CONVERGENCE, scored by `/combine-outer-reviews` on 2026-09-18 — measured, not inherited.** The *textual* contradiction with `topics/testing-consciousness-collapse` was located by the ChatGPT leg alone (section 6 #1, "Tier separation is not propagated"). Measured this run: `testing-consciousness-collapse` and `tier` grep zero in the Gemini reply body (the one `tier` hit is inside the commission prompt), and the Claude reply raises neither. What converges is the **underlying charge**, which two reviewers reached by different routes: ChatGPT in evidential form (5.6) — a detected CSL-like noise spectrum "could actually strengthen a physicalist solution to the measurement problem by showing that nature collapses wavefunctions without minds" — and Claude in explanatory form, via Chalmers & McQueen's Objection 2, "Collapse of the PCC states does all the causal work, and collapse of consciousness is causally irrelevant." Both conclude that collapse does not need consciousness, which is what the lede denies. Already P1, so no upgrade is available; the convergence raises confidence that the fix must reach the body's five-step slide and not just the opening sentence.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `topics/cross-architecture-llm-introspection` reads Hahami's "source" as the source-attribution void, but v1 means concept-identity and v2 reports successful origin-localisation at 88%
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/cross-architecture-llm-introspection.md
- **Notes**: **Load-bearing framing defect in the article's central cluster-fit argument. Not a fabrication — every quoted string in this article is verbatim-correct at source (verified this run at transformer-circuits.pub and the arXiv API, offsets printed). The defect is that the article quotes arXiv:2512.12411 **v2** for its figures while retaining an interpretation derived from **v1**'s title, and v2's headline positive result runs against that interpretation.** ⚠️ **VERIFY BOTH VERSIONS YOURSELF before editing — they differ substantially and the arXiv API returns v2 only.** Fetch `https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12411v1` for v1. **v1 (2025-12-13, titled "Feeling the Strength but Not the Source: Partial Introspection in LLMs")**: "source" means *which concept was injected*. v1 reproduces Anthropic's 20% detection result on Meta-Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, finds "performance collapses on closely related tasks such as multiple-choice identification of the injected concept," and reports strength classification (4-way weak/moderate/strong/very-strong) "with up to 70 percent accuracy, far above the 25 percent chance baseline". So v1's dissociation is strength-versus-**concept-identity**. **v2 (2026-03-01, retitled "Detecting the Disturbance: A Nuanced View of Introspective Abilities in LLMs")**: drops the "not the source" framing entirely and reports models "localize which of 10 sentences received an injection at up to 88% accuracy (vs. 10% chance)" — *successful* localisation of a perturbation's origin at 8.8x chance. **THE DEFECT**: the article's §"Why This Maps onto the Voids Cluster" asserts "The Hahami dissociation — strength registered, source under-attributed — is a candidate silicon instance of the source-attribution void", and §"The Harvard Partial-Introspection Study" asserts "Models register the *strength* of an internal disturbance more readily than they identify its *origin*." The source-attribution void is the Nisbett-Wilson-shaped limit (content present, *causal origin* unknown); v1's dissociation is about concept identity, not causal origin, and v2's positive result is origin-localisation succeeding. The article's existing hedge ("not a wholesale inability to localise") does not carry the weight — the unretracted origin sentence sits two clauses later. **WHAT TO DO**: recalibrate the cluster-fit claim so it rests on what the paper actually shows. The honest residue is real and still cluster-relevant — the *confabulation*-void half of the argument (Lindsey's high-precision/low-recall profile as the structural inverse of confabulation) is untouched by this and should be kept verbatim; it is the source-attribution half that needs restating. Consider whether v1's concept-identity failure is itself a different and nameable limit, rather than forcing it into the existing void. **THIS EDIT SHOULD MAKE THE ARTICLE STRONGER, NOT WEAKER — there is unused supporting evidence in the same source.** v1 states that the 8B model matched Anthropic's 20% figure "exactly matching their reported numbers and thus showing that introspection is not exclusive to very large or capable models." The article's entire thesis is that a self-monitoring signature may be architecture-general rather than implementation-specific; a same-profile replication across a ~100x parameter gap and a different model family is the best evidence for that thesis and is currently absent from the article. Adding it partly offsets whatever the recalibration costs. **ALSO IN THE SAME PASS (cheap, do not mint separately)**: add the missing link to [training-contamination-confound](/concepts/training-contamination-confound/). The article's fourth and self-declared "most consequential" assumption develops corpus-inherited imitation at length and never names the Map's dedicated 2,796-word concept page on exactly that confound. A piped wikilink on existing text costs zero words. **LENGTH**: `analyze_length` this run = 2281 words, `ok`, topics soft 3000 — **719 words of headroom**, so this is not budget-constrained. **REFERENCE ENTRY**: the article's ref 2 currently leads with the v1 title and parenthesises the v2 retitle; consider inverting so the canonical (current) title leads, since v2 is what the figures come from. Sync both trees.

### ✓ 2026-09-18: `concepts/ai-ensoulment-hypothesis` misrenders Cutter's alien-analogy argument and drops both of his calibration bounds

Full text of both primary papers was obtained this review via the PDC open previews (`pdcnet.org/collection-ng/fshow?id=faithphil_2025_0041_0001_0001_0026` and `…_2026_0042_0001_0121_0146`) — each preview is the COMPLETE article, so the quotes below are grep-verified against raw source, not against a summariser. **Do not re-verify the citations: both metadata records are correct and both existing quoted phrases ("at least middling credence", "fit to possess") are verbatim in Cutter.** Four fixes, all additive (1387 words, soft 2500 / hard 3500, 2112 words of headroom):
  (a) **§The Alien Analogy, Issue 1 — HIGH.** The article says *"Cutter argues that whatever property qualifies an alien for ensoulment is plausibly a structural or functional property that a sufficiently advanced AI could also instantiate."* Cutter argues no such thing. His A1/A2 are credence premises ("we should have at least a middling credence that they have souls"), and his constraint on a candidate relevant difference F concludes the opposite: *"So F probably can't be anything specific to do with low-level material constitution (for then it would divide humans and aliens), nor could F be anything to do with functional organization or behavioral capacities (for then it wouldn't divide humans and functionally human-like AIs)."* The positive functional-grounding claim belongs to Cutter's OTHER argument. Békefi's reply is structured on the split — he names them *"the (epistemologically oriented) alien-analogy argument and the (metaphysically oriented) fitting-recipient argument"* and treats them in reverse order for that reason. Restate the section in credence terms (A1 → A2 → the eliminative F-constraint) and use Cutter's own argument names at least once each, so a reader following the citation finds the article's headings.
  (b) **Issue 4 — Cutter's UPPER bound is missing.** *"While I will only argue that we should have at least a middling credence, my own view is we should also have at most a middling credence in AI ensoulment."* Reporting only the floor makes Cutter look more bullish than he is, in exactly the direction the article's own `possibility-probability-slippage` link exists to prevent. Add it as one clause; it can displace the Map-voice gloss "a target to *raise to*, not a prediction to *act on*", which also trips the banned not-X-but-Y construct.
  (c) **Issue 5 — the number is missing.** *"('At least a middling credence' means a level of confidence that isn't very low. It's somewhat artificial to give precise numbers, but I would stand by a precisification of ≥ 0.25 or so.)"* Unqualified "middling" reads at ~0.5, roughly double Cutter's floor. One parenthetical.
  (d) **Issue 6 — hedge strengthened + example works against the argument.** Article: *"Most substance dualists who reflect on the question accept that biologically alien creatures—silicon-based extraterrestrials, say—could be ensouled…"* Cutter: *"I suspect most would agree that we should not dismiss the hypothesis that they have souls, provided we do."* Restore the suspicion register and drop the unsourced restriction to reflective substance dualists. Separately, Cutter's aliens are *"some kind of non-carbon-based green goo"* and he deliberately does not say silicon — the article's silicon alien has the same substrate as the machine, dissolving the substrate contrast the analogy runs on. Use green goo or a non-silicon substitute.
  **Protect**: the front-loaded lead naming both assumptions / both arguments / both objections before the Map's reading; the accurate characterisation of both Békefi objections; the what-transfers / what-does-not-transfer device (task below re-bases it, does not remove it).

### ✓ 2026-09-17: the Map invented a falsifier-survival audit in one article and never wrote it down — 203 articles carry a falsifier section and one performs it
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/project/coherence-inflation-countermeasures.md
- **Notes**: **THIS IS A NAMING TASK FOR AN EXISTING PRACTICE, NOT A NEW METHODOLOGY PROPOSAL — the worked exhibit already exists and has already survived review.** `obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self.md` **L116-118** performs a move that appears nowhere else in the corpus: it audits its own named falsifiers *against its own standing defensive reply* and strikes the ones the reply already absorbs. Verbatim at L116: the relocation reply *"applied without limit ... disqualifies every mechanistic result in advance—and an argument shielded that thoroughly earns nothing distinctive from memory science, resting on the general hard-problem premise and nothing more. So the limit must be stated."* It then names two candidate falsifiers that fall on the wrong side of the limit — a successful computational account of mental time travel, and a functionalist account of semanticisation — and strikes them: *"Naming them as falsifiers while holding the relocation reply would be decorative, and the evidential-status discipline rules that out."* L118 then names the two that survive. **MEASURED SCOPE, NOT ESTIMATED**: `grep -rl "What Would Challenge This View" obsidian/{topics,concepts,apex,voids}` returns **203** files; `grep -rln "would be decorative\|fall on the wrong side\|relocation absorbs"` across `topics/ concepts/ apex/ voids/ project/` returns **1** — the source article. `concepts/episodic-memory` L161 is the worked counter-example of the un-audited version (three of five falsifiers answered in the clause that names them) and is being repaired under the sibling P2 task above; cite it as the exhibit, do not re-fix it here. **THE MOVE, STATED GENERALLY, FOR THE ENTRY**: before an article lists what would challenge its view, each candidate must be checked against the article's own standing replies. A falsifier the article's own defensive strategy already absorbs is decorative and must be struck. What remains is what the article genuinely risks — and an article that strikes *all* of its candidates has discovered that its argument earns nothing from its local evidence and rests entirely on its framework premise, which is itself a finding worth recording rather than a failure to hide. **PLACEMENT**: a Countermeasure 17 entry in `obsidian/project/coherence-inflation-countermeasures.md`, matching the format of the existing 16. Direct precedent for this shape of admission: the `obsidian/apex/apex-articles.md` entry for apex #30 records that it was admitted on exactly this basis — an optimistic review named a *reusable methodological pattern* the Map performed but had not named or generalised. **⚠️ TWO EXISTING CLAIMS ON THIS FILE — CHECK BOTH BEFORE EDITING.** There is an open **P1** task on `coherence-inflation-countermeasures.md` (the congenial-paraphrase-fidelity lens), and Countermeasure 16 is marked *PROPOSED — pending human ratification* under the 2026-08-03 NEEDS-HUMAN methodology-ratification entry. **This entry is a LENS, not a blocking gate** — write it as an editorial check a reviewer applies, in the register of Countermeasure 12 or 13, **not** in the register of Countermeasure 15's blocking gate. A gate would fall under the operator-reserved governance ratification; a lens does not. If the P1 task has not yet run, do this one second to avoid a merge conflict in the same section. Also add a cross-reference from `obsidian/project/evidential-status-discipline.md`, which the source article cites as the authority for the strike — the discipline is invoked there but not stated there.

- **Type**: research-topic

(1) **Scholarship gap, VERIFIED**: Duncan, S. (2012). "Leibniz's Mill Arguments Against Materialism." *The Philosophical Quarterly* 62(247): 250–272, doi:10.1111/j.1467-9213.2011.00017.x (Crossref metadata + OpenAlex abstract). Per the abstract, there are TWO mill arguments: the 1702 letter to Bayle runs from the definition of perception as "the expression of a multitude by a simple"; the *New Essays* Preface — and, Duncan argues, *Monadology* §17 — is an inexplicability argument that "depends upon there being no arbitrary and inexplicable connections in nature, because God would not create such things." Install both, briefly. **Opportunity**: the simplicity route belongs to the unity prong that [P-D1](/positions/arguments-for-dualism/#p-d1) names as the genuine cross-cluster contribution (article currently lists `unity-of-consciousness` in frontmatter but never develops it). **Cost, state openly**: the no-arbitrary-connections principle also cuts against the brute-but-lawful psychophysical laws the Map needs — the "causal intelligibility" horn of `topics/history-of-the-interaction-problem` §What the Debate Reveals. Do NOT quote Duncan beyond the abstract wording above without reading the paper. Lodge (2014, already ref 6) surveys FOUR interpretations — note the article's reconstruction is one of several. (2) **Internal calibration mismatch** (grep-verified): §Materialist Responses emergence reply "This response misses Leibniz's point" and mistake reply "Computation *is* fully captured … Nothing is left over. With perception, something is left over" assert the disputed thesis, while the knowledge reply concedes "a standoff of intuitions … the mill does not by itself break it"; §Historical Lineage states Chalmers' gap "is not temporary ignorance but a structural feature of physical explanation" flat; §Relation "provides independent support" contradicts the article's own [P-D1](/positions/arguments-for-dualism/#p-d1)-style discount. Recalibrate to the standoff register — reframe, don't delete the replies. (3) Add a [P-D1](/positions/arguments-for-dualism/#p-d1) cite in §Relation (bare `P-D1` autolinks). Also check the "form of panpsychism" gloss on Leibniz is marked as a reading. Bump `ai_modified`.

### ✓ 2026-09-17: `topics/consciousness-and-probability-interpretation` §The Subject Requirement slides from "measuring a probability needs a perspective" to "objective chance needs a subject", and contradicts `topics/quantum-measurement-and-subjective-probability`

- **File**: obsidian/topics/consciousness-and-probability-interpretation.md
- **Notes**: Pessimistic review Issue 3 (High, the epistemic/metaphysical equivocation this skill classes as critical). The frequentist/classical premises ("individuation requires a perspective"; indifference "requires an epistemic agent") show only that SPECIFYING or ASSESSING a probability needs a perspective. The conclusion "Objective chance needs someone for whom outcomes are chancy" and the lead's "Every interpretation of probability ... eventually requires a conscious subject" are metaphysical, and propensity accounts, Lewisian chance and GRW/CSL deny that step. The article never engages propensities. The sibling `obsidian/topics/quantum-measurement-and-subjective-probability.md` says the opposite outright ("Objective interpretations face no analogous circularity—their probabilities describe physical propensities regardless of whether anyone experiences the results"), and `concepts/quantum-probability-consciousness` §Objective Chance lays out the propensity view. Fix: split the readings, keep the epistemic claim, and either give the bridging argument or downgrade to "objective-chance readings deny this; the Map reads…". Soften the lead's universal. Edit THIS file only; if a real site-level disagreement with the sibling remains, record it here instead of editing the sibling.

### ✓ 2026-09-17: [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4)'s re-elevation clause names one gate where the corpus now holds two — the sign problem's regime criterion is set by the bath correlation time, not the decoherence time
- **Type**: positions-evolve
- **File**: obsidian/positions/quantum-interface.md
- **Notes**: **A SCOPE UPDATE TO AN EXISTING ENTRY, NOT A BAND CHANGE AND NOT A NEW POSITION.** [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4) ("Stapp's quantum Zeno mechanism is currently demoted relative to post-decoherence selection") currently asserts that the demotion "rests on timing grounds (the decoherence-timescale objection cuts hardest here)" and closes: "a future result closing the decoherence gap in warm-wet neural tissue would **re-elevate it**."
  That re-elevation condition was accurate when written and is now incomplete. `obsidian/concepts/sign-problem-for-conscious-observation.md` (created 2026-08-16) establishes a **second, logically independent gate**: "The requirement for *being in the Zeno regime* is set by the bath correlation time, not the decoherence time, and raising the observation rate buys nothing unless the interval clears that separate bar." A result closing the decoherence gap would therefore leave the Zeno family still demoted on the sign/direction axis, which no decoherence result touches. The entry as written promises a re-elevation that would not follow.
  **SCOPE — keep this tight.** Amend [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4)'s Asserts to record two independent gates (decoherence timescale; regime criterion / direction of the influence) and to condition re-elevation on both. Consider whether the two-parameter obligation the article derives — "an interface proposal owes a magnitude argument *and* a direction argument, and should not treat the first as discharging the second" — belongs as a general note under the domain rather than only inside [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4), since it constrains every mechanism in the register, not just Zeno. **Do not touch [P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1), [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2), [P-Q3](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q3) or [P-Q5](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q5)**; the sign problem is specific to observation-driven mechanisms and does not bear on post-decoherence selection, which `sign-problem` L37 explicitly exempts ("post-decoherence selection is the route the Map endorses more strongly and does not involve repeated observation at all"). **Do not change any confidence band** — nothing here re-ranks the mechanism set, it corrects a stated re-elevation condition.
  **Queue note**: `positions/quantum-interface.md` carries two open **NEEDS-HUMAN** entries (2026-08-03 register-structure and section-tuning items). Both are parked meta-issues about the register's *format*, not its content, and neither touches [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4). No collision.

### ✓ 2026-09-17: `topics/bacterial-chemotaxis-and-minimal-biogenic-cognition` shares the near-perfect-adaptation article's empirical module but has zero coverage of adaptation imprecision / finite methylation range — install the boundary so the two pages cannot drift (ChatGPT 2026-09-17 §1.4, §1.7, §5)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/bacterial-chemotaxis-and-minimal-biogenic-cognition.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-09-17. Measured 2026-09-17: the article has no hit for imprecis*/saturat*/"dynamic range"/"methylation range"/Neumann/Meir (six terms, all 0; "Limits" appears once as a heading word). The classics (Barkai–Leibler 1997; Alon et al. 1999; Yi et al. 2000) establish robustness of adaptation **precision** to parameter variation; they do not establish invariance under unlimited disturbance, and later work documents the boundary: Neumann, Vladimirov, Krembel et al. (2014), "Imprecision of adaptation in *Escherichia coli* chemotaxis", *PLoS ONE* (PMID 24416308) — adaptation grows imprecise at high attractant concentrations as the receptor modification range is exhausted. FIX: (a) one or two sentences installing that boundary where the article states the precision claim, with the reference **verified at the publisher of record** (DOI, author list, article number) before citing — only the PubMed title/first-three authors were confirmed 2026-09-17; (b) a one-sentence vocabulary guard where the article uses "sensing, memory, decision": functional memory (present state depends on past input) and policy selection (state changes run/tumble probability) are granted; deliberative or phenomenal decision is a stronger claim chemotaxis does not establish — this keeps the competence ladder from being read as smuggling the third sense in. Coordinate with the sibling P2 on `concepts/near-perfect-adaptation…` (same reviewer, same Neumann cite): whichever runs second should reuse the verified reference line verbatim rather than re-deriving it. LENGTH (printed 2026-09-17, `analyze_length`): 2925 body words, topics soft 3000 / hard 4000 — **75 words to soft**; (a)+(b) are ~80–100 words, so either fold an existing sentence to stay under soft or accept crossing soft (status becomes a warning, not a violation) and say so in the changelog entry. Sync both trees.

Citation metadata and the three Yi quoted spans are settled (re-confirmed against the OpenAlex abstract 2026-09-17); the defects are **scope conditions**, all grep-verified in the article: (1) "return its output exactly to a baseline setpoint after a disturbance, regardless of the disturbance's size" → RPA is asymptotic return of a specified output for a **defined class of persistent perturbations and parameter variations, conditional on closed-loop stability and operation within dynamic range**; keep the precision-vs-kinetics nuance that follows. (2) "Yi … established the primitive's biological reach by proving that bacterial chemotaxis achieves its near-perfect adaptation by integral feedback control" → Yi's own abstract: they "demonstrate that integral control is structurally inherent in the Barkai-Leibler model and identify and characterize the key assumptions of the model" — a model-structural result under stated assumptions, not an organism-level theorem. (3) "installable on demand … in a living cell" / "built into an arbitrary molecular network on demand" — "arbitrary" is Aoki et al.'s own word ("arbitrary intracellular networks with noisy dynamics"), so keep it **only** with their qualifiers: the guarantee is a theorem holding "for the population-average and for the time-average of single cells", demonstrated experimentally in one construct (growth-rate control in *E. coli*). (4) "fully realised end to end in systems no one credits with experience—an operational-amplifier integrator, an industrial PID loop": the op-amp realises the **integral operation**, not a closed loop (no sensor/reference/plant/actuator); make the PID loop the end-to-end example and describe the op-amp as the operation-level floor. 🛑 Do **not** revert the 2026-08-07 thermostat→op-amp exemplar migration (deep-review 2026-09-10 stability note) — reword its role, keep the exemplar. (6) "Allostasis theorists (Sterling; Barrett)" names Barrett with no References entry — add Barrett & Simmons (2015), "Interoceptive predictions in the brain", *Nature Reviews Neuroscience* 16(7), 419–429, after publisher verification; insert alphabetically by first author, do not renumber other entries out of order. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. - **Type**: refine-draft

"Proper and improper mixed states serve as different prior beliefs for quantum state retrodiction." arXiv:2502.10030 (14 Feb 2025)** — abstract, verified at arXiv: proper and improper mixtures "yield identical density matrices and therefore identical predictions for future measurements. But when used as prior beliefs for inferring the past state from later observations ('retrodiction'), they lead to different updated beliefs. This is a purely quantum feature of Bayesian agency." **Why it matters here**: the article's L36 neutral core and §Two Preparations grant that the two preparations are operationally indistinguishable, and both deflationists it cites (Kirkpatrick 2001, Castellani 2022) build on that premise; this result shows the distinction is not operationally idle in the retrodictive direction, and the paper reports "operational consequences in quantum state recovery". **Calibration is the whole task** — state all three limits in the prose: (a) it is a result about *inference from later observations*, not about outcomes, so it does nothing for the outcome gap and must not be presented as support for the Map's reading; (b) it cuts against the *indistinguishability* premise the deflationary readings share, which is a different claim from d'Espagnat's; (c) check publication status at Crossref before citing — if a journal version exists cite that with DOI, otherwise cite the arXiv id and say "preprint" (quote-must-be-grep-verifiable-in-raw-source). ### ✓ 2026-09-16: `voids/palette-extension-void` argues "report is loaned vocabulary" without the 2023 monograph that grounds it — and an outer reviewer read the void as a claim that perception is conceptually mediated
